 (i)  

 
  
PREFACE 
       I, the Chairman of the Committee on Subordinate Legislation, having 
been authorized by the Committee, do hereby present on its behalf, this 
Two Hundred and Fifty First Report of the Committee. 
2.         A total of 681 Notifications containing Rules /Regulations, issued 
under various provisions of Acts were laid on the Table of the Rajya  Sabha 
during its 257th  Session (18th July – 8thAugust, 2022). There had been delay 
in laying of 249 Notifications, out of which, 11 Notifications were laid 
without any delay statement from the concerned Minister giving reasons for 
the said delay. The Committee considered these delays and submits this 
Report. 
3. The Committee considered and adopted the draft 251st Report in its 
meeting held on  28th February, 2023. 
4.         The Committee’s observations/recommendations are printed in 
Chapter II (paras 3 and 4) in the Report. 
  
  
                                                                   DR. LAXMIKANT BAJPAYEE 
Chairman 
Committee on Subordinate Legislation, 
 RajyaSabha 
  
New Delhi; 
March  17, 2023 
  
   
  
  
(ii) 

1 
 
 
REPORT 
CHAPTER I 
1.         BACKGROUND 
      Rule 204 of the Rules of Procedure and Conduct of Business in the 
Council of States (Rajya Sabha) provides that there shall be a Committee on 
Subordinate Legislation to scrutinize and report to the Council whether the 
powers to make rules, regulations, bye -laws, sc hemes or other statutory 
instruments conferred by the Constitution or delegated by Parliament have 
been properly exercised within such conferment or delegation, as the case 
may be. 
1.2.      Mandate  
            Legislations passed by the Parliament and subsequently enacted 
after being assented to by the President of India, are implemented by the 
Executive i.e., various Ministries/Departments of Government of India. It is 
a well recognized principle th at the laws enacted by Parliament cannot be 
exhaustive enough to visualize each and every situation that may arise 
during the course of their implementation and to make provisions for it in 
the Act in anticipation. The Central Acts, therefore, if necessary ,  contain a 
provision authorizing the Government to frame detailed rules/regulations, 
etc. to enable it to implement the provisions of the Act. These 
rules/regulations framed in exercise of the powers conferred by an Act of 
Parliament are termed as Subordinate Legislation. 
Rule 209 of the Rules of Procedure and Conduct of Business in the 
Council of States lays down that after each rule, regulation, bye -law, 
scheme or other statutory instrument framed in pursuance of the 
Constitution/Act or the legislative functions delegated by the Parliament to 
a subordinate authority and which is required to be laid before Parliament, 
is so laid before the Council, the Committee shall, in particular consider: 
(i)   Whether the Order is in accord with the provisions of the  
 Constitution/Act  pursuant to which it is made; 
(ii)   Whether the Order contains matter which, in the opinion of 
 the Committee, should more properly be dealt within
 an Act of Parliament; 
(iii)   Whether the Order contains imposition of taxation; 
(iv)   Whether the Order directly or indirectly bars the jurisdiction 
 of the Courts; 

2 
 
(v)   Whether the Order gives retrospective effect to any of the 
 provisions in  respect of which the Constitution or the Act 
 does not expressly give any  such power; 
(vi)    Whether the Order involves expenditure from the 
 Consolidated Fund of India or the public revenues; 
(vii)  Whether the Order appears to make some unusual or 
 unexpected use of the powers conferred by the Constitution 
 or the Act pursuant to which it is made; 
(viii)  Whether there appears to have been unjustifiable delay in 
 its publication or laying before Parliament; and 
(ix)   Whether for any reason, the form or purport of the order 
 calls for any elucidation. 
 1.3       Statutory Requirement for laying  
             The Committee on Subordinate Legislation, Rajya  Sabha had, in its 
10th Report presented to the House on the 24 th May, 1971, inter-alia 
recommended, a standard laying formula to be incorporated not only in new 
legislation but also in ex isting Acts as and when Bills to amend them are 
brought before Parliament, which is reproduced below:- 
 
 "Every rule made by the Central Government under this Act shall 
be laid, as soon as may be after it is made, before each House of 
Parliament, while it is in session, for a total period of thirty days 
which may be comprised in one session or in two or more successive 
sessions, and if, before the expiry of the session immediately 
following the session or the successive sessions aforesaid,  both 
Houses  agree in  making any modification to  the rule  or both 
Houses agree that the rule should not be made, the rule shall 
thereafter have effect only in such modified form or be of no effect, 
as the case may be; so, however, that any such modification or 
annulment shall be without prejudice to the validity of anything 
previously done under that rule.” 
           Now, all statutes contain the above cited provision for making 
rules/regulations, for laying the same before both Houses of Parliament and 
for amending or annulling the same.  
1.4       Time limit for laying of rules/regulations 
             As per the recommendations contained in para 33 of the 1 st Report 
of the Committee, all the Orders including rules etc., are required to be laid 
on the Table of the House: - 
 
(a) within a period of 15 days after their publication in the  
 Gazette, if the House is in session; and 

3 
 
(b) if the House is not in session, within a period of 15 days  
 after the commencement  of  the  next  session. 
            However, in case of notifications relating to a state under the 
President’s rule, due to difficulties faced by certain Ministry, the Committee 
vide its recommendations contained in its 6 th Report (para 17 -19) relaxed 
this time period to 30 days  from the date of publication of the rules, orders, 
notifications etc., in the State Gazette. 
 1.5       Motion for annulment or modification of rules/regulations laid 
on the Table of the House 
             As per recommendations of the Committee contained  in its 10 th 
Report ( cited at para 1.3 ante), after the rules/regulations are laid on the 
Table of the House, Members can move motion for modification or 
annulment of the rules/regulations. The statutory period of laying of 30 days 
might be completed in on e session or two or more successive sessions. 
Further, as per the recommendation of the Committee contained in its 75 th 
Report (Para 2.5), reproduced below:- 
  
"The Committee agrees with the view of the Committee on Subordinate 
Legislation Lok Sabha (Seventh Lok Sabha) expressed in its 20th Report, 
that laying on the Table of all Orders' in pursuance of powers delegated 
by Parliament is very significant, as it affords an opportunity to 
Members of Parliament if they so desire to move any amendment or 
modification to such 'order'; including a Motion for their annulment.”  
            Hence, a reading of both these recommendations together indicates 
that Members have a right to suggest/move motion for 
annulment/modifications in the 'Order' for a time period til l one additional 
session immediately following the session in which the period of 30 days is 
completed. If the modification or annulment is carried by the Houses of 
Parliament, the rules/regulations stand annulled or modified to the extent 
agreed to by the Houses of Parliament. 
 1.6       Role of the Committee on Subordinate Legislation, Rajya  
Sabha with regard to unjustifiable delay in laying 
             One of the important functions of the Committee, according to Rule 
209 (viii), is to see whether there  has been unjustifiable delay in the 
publication or laying of the rules/regulations before the Parliament. The 
Committee monitors the laying of the rules/regulations etc. on the Table of 
Rajya Sabha during its Session through its Reports. This practice started 
with the 131 st Report of the Committee on Statutory Orders laid on the 
Table of the Rajya  Sabha during its 190 th Session, presented to the House 

4 
 
on 15th December, 2000. During the meeting of the Committee held on 11 th 
December, 2000, to adopt the s aid report, the Committee decided that 
henceforth, it will regularly scrutinize the  notifications laid  on  the 
Table  of  Rajya Sabha from time to time and present a report to the Council 
in respect of each Session. This practice has continued since then  and the 
present report is in pursuance of the said decision of the Committee. 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  

5 
 
CHAPTER II 
LAYING OF STATUTORY ORDERS ON THE 
TABLE  OF RAJYA SABHA 
               During the 25 7th  Session of the Rajya  Sabha, ( 18th July  – 8th 
August, 2022 ), 681 (Six Hundred Eighty-One) Statutory 
Orders/Notifications relating to Rules/Regulations/Schemes/Bye -laws etc., 
framed under the Constitution/Acts of Parliament, were laid on the Table of 
the Council by various Ministries/Departments of the Government of India. 
The details of the Gazette Notifications in which these were published and 
the date of their laying on the Table of the Rajya  Sabha have been indicated 
Ministry-wise, in the Statement placed at Annexure. 
2.1       While scrutinizing these Orders, the Committee noted that a 
majority of  the Ministries/Departments of the Government of India,  have 
ensured timely laying of the Gazette Notifications brought out by them. The 
annexed statement also indicates the period of delay in cases where 
Notifications have not been laid as per the time schedule prescribed by the 
Committee. During the 257th Session, out of 681 Notifications that were laid 
on the Table of Rajya  Sabha, 432 were laid within the stipulated time, and 
249 Notifications were delayed. Further, out of these 249 delayed 
Notifications, 64 Notifications were delayed by a period of upto 11 months; 
23 Notifications were delayed by periods ranging between 1 year to 2 years; 
and 162 Notifications were delayed by more than 2 years. The Commi ttee, 
however, observes that 238 (95.58%) out of these 249 delayed 
Notifications, was accompanied by delay statement from the concerned 
Minister and 11 (4.41%) Notifications did not have any delay statement 
from the concerned Minister enclosed along with the Notifications. The 
bulk of delayed Notifications were from the Ministry of Fisheries, Animal 
Husbandry & Dairying (Department of Animal Husbandry and Dairying)  
with 107 Notifications, which comes to around 42.97% of the total delayed 
Notifications, Ministry of Road Transport and Highways (30 delayed 
Notifications) which comes to 12% of the total delayed Notifications, and 
Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change  (27 delayed 
Notifications) which comes to 10.84% of the total delayed Notificat ions,  
some other Ministries also had a large number of delayed Notifications e.g.,  
Ministry of External Affairs (24 delayed Notifications); (Department of 
Revenue) Ministry of Finance (17 delayed Notifications); Ministry of 
Health and Family Welfare (Department of Health and Family Welfare)  (8 
delayed Notifications); Ministry of Textiles (7 delayed Notifications); 
Ministry of Ag riculture and Farmers Welfare  (Department of Agriculture 
and Farmers Welfare ) (6 delayed Notifications)  and Ministry of Finan ce 
(Department of Financial Services ) and Ministry of Personnel, Public 
Grievances and Pensions (Department of Personnel and Training (both are 3 
delayed Notifications).  Hence, the Committee has examined these data in 
detail and important issues have been highlighted for guidance of the 

6 
 
Ministries/Departments for the future. The following are the Ministry -wise 
details of the Notifications that were laid during the Session , which were 
delayed:-

2.3      The Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers Welfare  (Department of 
Agriculture and Farmers Welfare ) laid 11 Notifications, out of which, 6 
Notifications were delayed by periods ranging from 5 to 11 months. While 
5 Notifications were with delay statement s appended to them. One 
Notification was without delay statement appended to it. The reasons for the 
delay given by the Ministry are as under:- 
 
"Department of Agriculture and Farmers Welfare has issued the 
Notification No. G.S.R. No. 620 (E) dated 8 th September, 2021 under the 

7 
 
Insecticides Act, 1968 for amendment in the Insecticides Rules, 1971.  In 
terms of Section 36(3) of the Insecticides Act, 1968 every rule made by the 
Central Government under the said Act shall be laid, as soon as may be 
after it is made, before each House of Parliament.  Therefore, the said 
notification was required to be laid on the Table of both the House of 
Parliament during the Session held in March -April, 2022.  However the 
notification could not be laid on the Table of Rajya Sabha within the 
stipulated period for the following reasons:- 
 The Notification G.S.R. No. 620 (E) dated 8 th September, 2021 was 
sent for lay ing on the table of both the House of Parliament vide letter 
dated 31st March, 2022.  Rajya Sabha Secretariat has informed that the said 
notification was listed for laying on the Table of Rajya Sabha on 8 th April, 
2022.  However the House was adjourned sin e-die earlier than scheduled 
i.e. on 07.04.2022 and Secretariat returned the copes of notification.  
Therefore the notification could not be laid on the Table of Rajya Sabha 
during the Session held in March-April, 2022. 
Hence the delay." 
 
II "Department of  Agriculture and Farmers Welfare has issued the 
Notification No. S.O. 153 (E) dated 12 January, 2022 under the Insecticides 
Act, 1968 for inclusion of new insecticides to the schedule to the 
Insecticides, 1968. In terms of the recommendations of the Commit tee on 
Subordinate Legislation made in para 66 of their 4 th  Report (3 rd  Lok 
Sabha), all rules or orders, as soon as may be after it is made required to 
laid, before each House of Parliament within a period of 15 days after their 
publication in the Gazett e. Therefore, the said notification was required to 
be laid on the Table of both the Houses of Parliament during the Session 
held in March -April, 2022. However the notification could not be laid on 
the Table of Rajya Sabha within the stipulated period for the following 
reasons: 
The Notification S.O. 153 (E) dated 12 th January, 2022 was sent for 
laying on the table of both the Houses of Parliament vide letter dated 31 st 
March, 2022. Rajya Sabha Secretariat has informed that the said 
notification was listed for laying on the Table of Rajya Sabha on 8 th  April, 
2022. However, the House was adjourned sine -die earlier than scheduled 
i.e., on 07.04.2022 and Secretariat return ed the copies of notification. 
Therefore, the notification could not be laid on the Table of Rajya Sabha 
during the Session held in March-April, 2022. 
 Hence the delay." 
III Department of Agriculture and Farmers Welfare have issued the 
Notification No. G.S .R No. 39 (E) dated 24 th  January, 2022 under the 

8 
 
Insecticides Act, 1968 for amendment in the Insecticides Rules, 1971. In 
terms of Section 36(3) of the Insecticides Act, 1968 every rule made by the 
Central Government under the said Act shall be laid, as s oon as may be 
after it is made, before each House of Parliament. Therefore, the said 
notification was required to be laid on the Table of both the Houses of 
Parliament during the Session held in March -April, 2022. However the 
notification could not be laid  on the Table of Rajya Sabha within the 
stipulated period for the following reasons: 
The Notification G.S.R No. 39(E) dated 24th  January, 2022 was sent 
for laying on the table of both the Houses of Parliament vide letter dated 
31st March, 2022. Rajya Sabh a Secretariat has informed that the said 
notification was listed for laying on the Table of Rajya Sabha on 8 th  April, 
2022. However, the House was adjourned sine -die earlier than scheduled 
i.e. on 07.04.2022 and Secretariat returned the copies of notifica tion. 
Therefore, the notification could not be laid on the Table of Rajya Sabha 
during the Session held in March-April, 2022. 
Hence the delay." 
IV & V  "Statement giving reasons for delay in laying the papers in 
the Parliament. 
 In exercise of the powers conferred by Section 19 and Section 20 of 
Coconut Development Board 1979, the Central Government has notified 
CDB (Amendment) Rules, 2021,  CDB  (Amendment) Regulations, 2021 and 
CDB Employees (Conditions of Service) Amendment Regulations, 2021. 
The DCB (A mendment) Rules, 2021 and CDB (Amendment) Regulations, 
2021 were notified in the Gazette of India (Extraordinary) on 17.01.2022 
vide G.S.R. 17 (E) and 19.01.2022 vide S.O. 277 (E) respectively. However, 
CDB Employees (Conditions of Service) Amendment Regul ations, 2022 was 
notified in the Gazette of India (Extraordinary) on 27.05.2022 vide S.O. 
2430 (E). The Budget session of Parliament ended on 08.04.2022. Papers 
are to be sent to Lok Sabha/Rajya Sabha Secretariat at least 3 working days 
before the day on w hich these are proposed to be laid. As the CDB 
Employees (Conditions of Service) Amendment Regulations, 2022 was 
notified well after the end of Budget Session, therefore, these could not be 
laid in the Budget Session, 2022."

laying on the table of Rajya Sabha/Lok Sabha."

2.7 The Ministry of Corporate Affairs laid 29 Notifications during the 
Session, o ut of which one Notification was delayed by 46 months and a 
delay statement was appended thereto. The Ministry  had cited the reason 
for the delay as under:- 
"As per provisions of section 469(4) of the Companies Act, 2013, the 
notification relating to G.S.R. 895(E) dated 19.09.2018 on the Companies 
(Corporate Social Responsibility Policy) Amendment Rules, 2018 was 
required to be laid before both the Houses of Parliament during Winter 
Session, 2018. 
 
 The above notification after publication in e -gazette of the 
Government of India could not be laid befo re both the Houses of 
Parliament during Winter Session, 2018 of the Parliament due to 
inadvertent oversight. The delay caused in laying of notification is sincerely 
regretted and may kindly be condoned."

2.8       The Ministry of Culture laid only one Notification during the 
Session, which was  delayed by 18  months and a delay statement was also 
appended to it. The reason given by the Ministry for the delay is as under:- 
 
 "The amendment was carried out by publishing it in the Gazette of 
India, Part II, Section 3, Sub-section (i) on 28th January, 2021. 
 Therefore, as per the provisions of sub -section (4) of Section 38 of 
the Ancient Monuments and Archaeological Sites and Remains Act, 1958, 
the amendment notification was processed to be laid in both the Houses of 
the Parliament during the Previous Session. 
 But, the notification could not be laid in the Hoses during the 
Previous Session because of certain administrative reasons. 
 Now, the notification is being laid in both the Houses of the 
Parliament during the Present Session."

2.12    The Department of Financial Services , Ministry of Finance  laid 18 
Notifications, out of which, 3 Notifications were delayed by periods ranging 
from 5 months to 1 years and 8 months, and one delayed   Notification had 
no delay statement appended to it, while the remaining two Notifications 
had delay statement attached to them. . The reason given by the Ministry for 
the delay is  as under:- 
"Sub-section (3) of Sec tion 48 of the Life Insurance Corporation 
Act, 1956, stipulates that every rule made by the Central Government under 
the said Act, shall be laid before each House of the Parliament. The Life 
Insurance Corporation of India General (Amendment) Rules, 2022 wh ich 
was notified on 03.03.2022 was to be laid before both the Houses of 
Parliament in the Budget Session (post adjournment on 11 th February. 
2022) was held during 14 th  March, 2022 to 8 th  April, 2022. In this 
connection, it is submitted that inadvertently the Life Insurance 
Corporation of India General (Amendment) Rules, 2022 not be laid before 
both the Houses of Parliament in the Budget Session within the prescribed 
period. 
The delay in laying the aforesaid Rules before the Parliament is 
sincerely regretted and it is requested that the same may please be 
condoned."

Rules, 2017" was issued under Notification No. 12/2017 -SERVICE TAX

VII The laying of following notifications vide serial number 1 to 3 in the 
table below issued under the Central Excise Act, 1944 has been delayed 
inadvertently. The Notifications were issued at the time when the entire 
indirect taxes syst em was being overhauled, culminating into introduction 
of Goods and Services Tax with effect from 1.07.2017. under the one -nation 
one - Tax vision of the government. At this historic juncture, the major focus 
was on the smooth roll-out and implementation of GST in coordination with 
the state governments with the spirit of cooperative federalism. While, a 
large number of indirect taxes were subsumed under GST, the legacy law of 
Central Excise Act, 1944 was repealed save the items in Entry 84 of List - 1 
of the Seventh Schedule of the Constitution of India. The advent of GST also 
led to changes in the policy sections/wings of CBIC along with 
consequential re -distribution of officers and files within the ministry. A 
large number of notification under GST regime were issued on 30.06.2017 
which were laid on the table of the House in due course. In the process, the 
laying of notifications vide serial  number 1 to 3in table below, pertaining 
to central excise, was inadvertently missed due to oversight. The 
inadvertent delay is deeply regretted and has been noted for strict 
compliance in future. They are being placed on the table of the Lok Sabha 
and Rajya Sabha.

XI The Notifications upon their issuance are required to be placed 
before both houses of Parliament for a period of thirty days. 
The Exchange Rate Notification and Tariff Value Notifications are issued in 
a routine manner. These Notifications are issued very frequently with 
minimum of 4 Notifications being issued every month. The earlier practice 
was to send as many as Notif ications in the same session and the ones 
which could not be sent during the same session, the same are laid before 
the 02 Houses of Parliament in the next session. 

21 
 
This Notification could not be laid during the last session as the total 
sittings in the se ssion were only 29. Since it has been administratively 
difficult to lay such Notifications which are issued during any short session, 
the past practices has been to lay the same in the next session. However, it 
is ensured that maximum number of notificatio ns issued during a session 
are laid during the same session. 
The same are being tabled now during the monsoon session immediately 
after the budge session. 
XII This notification was issued under the Customs Act, 1962 by Central 
Board of Indirect Taxes and Customs. The laying of the notification has 
been delayed inadvertently. The inadvertent delay is regretted and duly 
noted for strict compliance of timelines in future. The same is now being 
placed on the table of the Lok Sabha and Rajya Sabha.

DELAY STATEMENT 
II Ministry of Fisheries, Animal Husbandry and Dairying, Department 
of Animal Husbandry and Dairying is regulating import of Livestock and 
Livestock products under the Livestock Importation Act, 1898 (Act No. 9 of 
1898) and Livestock Importation (Amendment) Act, 2001 (Act No. 28 of 
2001). Accordingly, the various rules / regulations / procedures for import 
of Livestock and livestock products into the country, as annexed herein, are 
being framed and notified time to time to make better provision for the 
regulation of the importation of live -stock and livestock products which is 
liable to be affected by infectious or contagious disorders. 
In the said act, no clause/provision has been specified, for laying the 
notifications on the table of both the Houses of Parliament. However, as 
desired by the Hon'ble Minister of Parliamentary Affairs, vide D.O. No . 
2/3/2016-ME, dated November 10, 2021, all the available notifications, 
framed and notified under the said act are being laid on the table of both 
the Houses of Parliament. 
In view of the above facts, the delay in submission for being tabled 
on floor of the house may please be condoned."

RECOMMENDATIONS/OBSERVATIONS 
           At the outset, the Committee would like to commend the 
Ministries/Departments of the Government of India for putting in a 
good effort in laying a total of 681 Notifications during the 25 7th 
Session. However, out of the Notificatio ns laid, 249 Notifications were 
delayed and 185 of them were delayed by a period of more than 1 1 
months, some of which   were delayed by  as much as 37 years and 03 
months, which is totally unreasonable and need deeper introspection by 
the concerned Ministries/Department. 
            The Committee observes that 64 Notifications, which account 
for (25.70%) of all the delayed Notifications were late by more than 1 0 
months; 8  Notifications, which come to ( 3.21%) of all the delayed 
Notifications, were late by 10 months ; 2 Notifications (0.80%) were late 
by 9 months; 7 Notifications ( 2.81%) were late by 8 months; 5 
Notifications ( 2.00%) were late by 7 months; 15 Notifications 6.02%) 
were late by 6 months; 20 Notifications (8.03%) were late by 5 months; 
and 1 Notification (0.40%) was late by 4 months. This indicates that on 
as average, 36.56% of the Notifications that were laid on the Table of 
Rajya Sabha during the 25 7th Session were delayed. This shows that 
though most of the Ministries/Departments have put in a good effort, 
more needs to be done to obviate delay in laying these Notifications on 
the Table of Rajya Sabha.  
            The Committee observes that  some of the Ministries, which h ad 
multiple delayed Notifications, e.g., Ministry of Agriculture and 
Farmers Welfare  (Department of Agriculture and Farmers  Welfare); 
Ministry of AYUSH; Ministry of Commerce and Industry (Department 
of Commerce ); Ministry of Electronics and Information Technology; 
Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change;  Ministry of 
External Affairs; Ministry of Finance (Department of Financial 
Services and Department of Revenue ); Ministry of Fisheries, Animal 
Husbandry & Dairying (Department of Animal Husband ry and 
Dairying); Ministry of Health and Family Welfare (Department of 
Health and Family Welfare);  Ministry of Home Affairs; Ministry of 
Personnel, Public Grievances and Pensions (Department of Personnel 
and Training);  Ministry of Road Transport and Highways; and  
Ministry of Textiles etc. need to review the procedure followed by them 
for laying of their Gazette Notifications and if necessary,  issue 
instructions to all  their Departments and  subordinate/attached 
offices/organizations, impressing upon t hem the importance of laying 
all notifications within the stipulated time and if there is any delay, then 
to attach a delay statement, explaining cogent reasons for the delay and 
to take steps to obviate such delays in future. Among the 
Ministries/Departments, which had delayed Notifications, certain 

42 
 
Notifications were delayed by huge amount time as much as 37 years 
and 3 months, 30 years and 1 month to 30 years and 9 months , 1 year 
and 1 month to 26 years and 11 months etc.,  which the Committee feels 
is totally unacceptable. The Committee, after considering the above 
data, makes the following recommendations and directs that 
these  recommendations may be circulated to all the 
Ministries/Departments for strict complianc e. The Ministry of 
Parliamentary Affairs may take necessary step to co -ordinate with 
other Ministries/Departments on this serious issue. 
  
4.         RECOMMENDATIONS OF THE COMMITTEE 
 a) Delay in Laying of Orders/Rules/Regulations 
     
4.1   As observed in the preceding section, the Committee notes that 249 
delayed notifications laid by the Ministries/Departments of 
Government of India, during the 25 7th Session of Rajya  Sabha, were 
delayed by various amounts of time. The Committee would like to draw 
the attention of few Ministries, which laid large number of 
Notifications  on the Table of Rajya  Sabha after a considerable delay 
e.g., Agriculture and Farmers Welfare (Department of Agriculture and 
Farmers Welfare)  – 6 Notifications delayed out of a total of 11 
Notifications and all delay  Notifications were delayed   by periods 
between ranging from  5 months to 11 months; AYUSH – 2 
Notifications laid on the Table  and  both were delayed   by periods 
between 7 months to 1 year  and 2 months; Environment, Forest and 
Climate Change – 26 Notifications were delayed, out of a total of 44 
Notifications and these were delayed   by periods ranging from 5 
months to 6 years and 3 months; External Affairs – 24 Notifications, all 
Notifications were delayed   by periods ranging from 6 months to 7 
years and 9 months; Finance (Department of Financial Services ) – 3 
Notifications were delayed, out of a total of 18 Notifications and all 3 
Notifications were delayed by periods ranging from 5 months to 1 year 
and 8 months; Department of Revenue – 17 Notifications were delayed, 
out of a total of 188 Notifications and these delayed Notifications were 
delayed by 5 months  to 11 years; Fisheries, Animal Husbandry & 
Dairying (Department of Animal Husbandry and Dairying)  – 107 
Notifications and all were delayed by huge time periods i.e.,  12 
Notifications were delayed by  periods ranging from   21 years to 37 
years and 3 months; 82 Notifica tions  were delayed by 5 years to 20 
years and 11 months, while 10 Notifications delayed by 1 years  and 1 
month to 4 years ;  Health and Family Welfare  (Department of Health 
and Family Welfare) – 8 Notifications were delayed, out of a total of 14 
Notifications, delayed by period ranging from 5 months to 1 year and 3 

43 
 
months; Mines – 4 Notifications were delayed, out of a total of 11 
Notifications, delayed by period s ranging from 5 months to 1 year and 
4 months; The Ministry of Personnel, Public Grie vances and Pensions 
(Department of Personnel and Training) – 3 Notifications were delayed, 
out of a total of 9 Notifications and these delayed Notifications were 
delayed by 9  months  to 1  year; Road Transport and Highways – 30 
Notifications were delayed, out of a total of 76 Notifications and these 
delayed Notifications were delayed by periods ranging from 4 months  
to 1  year ; and Textiles – 7 Notifications, all were delayed by periods 
ranging from 6 months to 1 year. The Committee would like to draw 
special attention to the delays of Ministries of Environment, Forest and 
Climate Change , External Affairs , Department of Revenue, Fisheries, 
Animal Husbandry & Dairying (Department of Animal Husbandry and 
Dairying), were th e amount of delays were huge i.e., beyond 2 years 
and many of their Notifications were delayed by as much as 37 years 
and 3 months, which is quite unacceptable. The Committee feels that 
such inordinate delays reflect the callous attitude of the concerned 
Ministries and defeat the very purpose of laying such Notifications on 
the Table of the House. More importantly, this could open unwanted 
scenarios as Members have a right to annulment of the Notifications 
laid, wi thin the time period specified in the layi ng provisions of the 
relevant Act.  Hence, such motions for amendment/annulment of a 
Notification which is 37 years old would create an unwanted crisis. The 
Committee, therefore, once again, impresses upon all 
Ministries/Departments in general and specific ally those mentioned 
above, to avoid such delays in the future. The Committee feels that such 
delays are totally avoidable and in violation of its recommendations 
contained in its 131st Report wherein it had recommended:- 
         
        ―Orders required to be laid before the House should be laid (i) if 
the House is not in Session, during the Session immediately following 
the date of publication of the Order in the Official Gazette and (ii) if 
the House is in Session, on the date of the publication of the Order, 
during its continuance and in case the time lag between the date of 
publication and the date of the closing of the Session is less than 15 
clear days, before the expiry of the Session immediately following the 
said Session. All the Ministries/Depart ments of Government of India 
should henceforth comply with this schedule in the matter of laying of 
the Orders issued under various Acts of Parliament on the Table of the 
Rajya Sabha.‖ 
            The Committee, while reiterating its above recommendations,  
once again recommends that the Ministries of Agriculture and Farmers 
Welfare (Department of Agriculture and Farmers Welfare) ; Ayush; 
Commerce and Industry (Department of Commerce); Corporate 
Affairs; Culture; Environment, Forest and Climate Change ; External 

44 
 
Affairs; Finane (Department of Financial Services and Department of 
Revenue); Fisheries, Animal Husbandry & Dairying (Department of 
Animal Husbandry and Dairying) ; Health and Family Welfare; Mines; 
Personnel, Public Grievances and Pensions (Department of Personnel 
and Training) ; Road Transport and Highways ; Textiles; and Women 
and Child Development should ensure that all its notifications are laid 
on the Table of Rajya Sabha within the prescribed time of 15 days. 
 b) Non-attachment of ‗Delay Statements‘  to the delayed Notifications 
laid 
 4.2       The Committee feels that the most disconcerting point is that 11 
Notifications, out of a total 249 delayed Notifications, were laid during 
this Session, without any delay statement appended to them, which goes 
against the recommendation of the Committee contained in its earlier 
Report (135th Report) reproduced below:- 
        ―The Committee has, time and again, emphasized that it is 
essential that in case there is a delay in the laying of Statutory Orders, 
the concerned Ministry/Department should invariably append a delay 
statement along with the Notifications being laid so as to apprise the 
Parliament of the causes of delay. The Committee desires all 
Ministries/Departments to take a note of this, since the inten tion is that 
through the process of formulation of a delay statement, the Ministry is 
able to introspect, identify bottlenecks and devise remedial measures‖. 
           The Committee recommends that all the Ministries/Departments 
must adhere to the time sc hedule for laying of Notifications as 
prescribed in Committee‘s 131 st Report and in case, the delay in laying 
of Notifications is unavoidable, the Ministry should attach a delay 
statement from their Minister citing cogent reasons for the delay. The 
delay s tatement gives an opportunity, to the Ministry to clarify the 
reasons for the delay in laying of the Notification but also a reason for 
the Ministry/Department to introspect and improve their functioning. 
In case this longstanding recommendation of the Com mittee is not 
followed, then the accountability of the Ministries to the Parliament 
and the Constitutional Mandate of the Parliament to monitor the 
functioning of the Executive would become a futile exercise and the 
Committee has every right to take this matter very seriously. 
 
c) Unnecessary Delay Statements 
 
 The Committee notes that on many instances, the Ministries/ 
Departments have enclosed undue delay statements with the 
Notifications which are actually not delayed. The Committee is not 
happy at the casual and perfunctory manner in which the Ministry has 

45 
 
treated to recommendations with delay statement. The 
Ministries/Departments has recommended that delay statement with 
substantive reasons should be enclosed with the only those Notifications 
who that are delayed. 
 
 d) Format of Delay Statements 
4.3    The Co mmittee observes that many of the 
Ministries/Departments, whose Notifications were delayed, ha ve not 
made any sincere effort to identify the reasons for the delay in laying 
them by citing reasons like "curtailment/sudden closure of Session, due 
to Covid pandemic, sine-die adjournment", "due to inadvertent 
oversight", 'inadvertent administrative reasons", "due to limited 
business days and requisite administrative formalities", 
"inadvertently",  "due to dela yed receipt of printed copies", "took 
some time to collect the papers and complete the procedural 
formalities" etc. Certain Ministry stated in the delay statement that the 
House was adjourned sine -die earlier than scheduled , and hence could 
not be laid. The Committee, therefore, recommend s that the 
Ministries/Departments should keep track  of the Parliament Session 
and send their Notification for layi ng on the Table of the House and 
efforts should be made to send the Notifications during the early part of 
the Session so as to avoid last mi nute rush and delays. The Committee, 
therefore, strongly recommends that the Ministries/Departments 
should cite cogent, logical and self explanatory reasons for the delay in 
the laying of Notifications so that accountability of the 
Ministries/Departments t o the Parliament is maintained   in its true 
letter and spirit and to ensure that, it does remain a mere formality. 
The Committee would like to emphasize that the purpose of this Report 
is not a punitive exercise but a fact finding process to improve the 
procedure followed by the Ministries/Departments though the 
Committees recommendations and make the process smoother and 
relevant. The Committee , therefore,  recommends that all the 
Ministries/Departments should put in more effort to ensure timely 
delivery of these Notifications and also to follow the guidelines issued 
by the Table Office, Rajya  Sabha Secretariat to all the 
Ministries/Departments of Government of India on the eve of each 
Session, so as to avoid such difficulties and also to facilitate the la ying 
of Gazette Notifications on the Table of Rajya  Sabha smoothly. The 
Committee would also like to re -iterate its earlier recommendations 
contained in para2(xii) of its 189th Report, which is reproduced below:- 
              ―The Committee is not happy a t the casual manner in which 
the Ministries have treated its recommendations with regard to the 
delay in the laying of the Notifications despite the Committee‘s 
reiteration in all its reports. The Committee takes serious note of the 

46 
 
fact that, in most case s, the Ministries fail to append delay statements 
explaining the reasons for the delay. Therefore, the Committee 
reiterates, that the Ministries should take proactive measures so as to 
avoid any delay, whatsoever, in the laying of the Notifications and tha t 
the Committee is unable to accept the flimsy grounds given by the 
Ministries regarding the reasons for delay.‖ 
           The Committee, therefore, recommends these 
Ministries/Departments to not only ensure timely laying of 
Notifications but also follow the practice of enclosing a delay statement 
in case(s) of delays. 
e)  Need  for proper Gazette Notification Numbering (G.S.R./S.O. No.) 
and nomenclature in Short Titles  
 4.4         The Committee notes that many of the Gazette Notifications 
were published without their unique G.S.R./S.O. Nos. and the file 
numbers have been  used as Notification numbers, which is against the 
guidelines issued by the Government of India in this regard. The 
Committee would also like to reiterate its earlier recommendation s to 
notify amendments to rules/regulations with proper nomenclature in 
the short title e.g.,  1st amendment or 2 nd amendment etc. The 
Committee, therefore, recommends that:- 
i All Gazett e Notification must have a unique 
 G.S.R./S.O./S.R.O. number and giving file number as 
 Gazette Notification numbers  must be discontinued; 
ii The short title of amendments to rules/regulations etc. 
 must indicate whether the amendment is 1 st or 2 nd or so 
 on for easy identification.  
 
 
f) EARLIER IMPORTANT RECOMMENDATIONS OF THE 
COMMITTEE ON SUBORDINATELEGISLATION, RAJYA SABHA 
 4.5       The Committee in its earlier recommendations, for strict 
compliance by all the Ministries/Departments of the Government of India, 
had stated that:- 
  
Formula for laying of Statutory Rules 
            The Committee approve the following draft revised formula for 
laying of Statutory Rules before both Houses of Parliament which the 

47 
 
Ministry of Law and Justice propose to incorp orate in all future 
legislation which provide for making of rules by the Central 
Government:— 
  
        "Every rule made by the Central Government under this Act 
shall be laid, as soon as may be after it is made, before each House 
of Parliament, while it is in session, for a total period of thirty days 
which may be comprised in one session or in two or more 
successive sessions, and if, before the expiry of the session 
immediately following the session or the successive sessions 
aforesaid, both Houses agree  in making any modification in the 
rule or both Houses agree that the rule should not be made, the 
rule shall thereafter have effect only in such modified form or be of 
no effect, as the case may be; so, however that any such 
modification or annulment shal l be without prejudice to the 
validity of anything previously done under that rule." 
        
   The Committee recommends that the revised formula should be 
incorporated not only in new legislation but also in the existing Acts as 
and when Bills to amend th em are brought before Parliament. The 
Ministry should also incorporate the new formula in the Bills pending 
before Parliament. This will, in the opinion of the Committee, bring 
about uniformity in the matter expeditiously. 
 
(10th Report) 
(Presented on 24th May, 1971) 
 
 
Incorporating corrections and amendments in the order 
 
             ―The Committee desires that for the convenience of the public 
and Members, Ministries should henceforth send copies of 'order' duly 
authenticated by the Ministers concerned, incorporating therein all the 
corrections and amendments issued till d ate of laying such 'orders' 
instead of laying the uncorrected copy along with corrigenda. The 
Committee feels that to obviate any legal or technical difficulty, 
Ministries should examine whether there is any necessity of assigning a 
separate G.S.R. No. to a corrigendum at the time of its publication in 
the Gazette and whether such a corrigendum should not be published 
without assigning any G.S.R. No. The Committee would, therefore, like 
to impress upon all Ministries of the Government of India that the 
present practice of laying the rules without incorporating corrigenda in 
the body of the rules is not a healthy one. The Committee hopes that the 
Ministries will ensure supply of only corrected copies of the rules for 

48 
 
laying on the Table so that they depict a true and correct picture and 
serve the desired purpose." 
            (7th Report) 
(Presented on 12th May, 1969) 
 
Assigning distinctive number to each Notification 
        ―In the opinion of the Committee, every notification should be 
assigned a distinctive number in order to give it a separate identity and 
similarly, no notification should be published/laid which does not carry 
a notification number. The Committee expects all 
Ministries/Departments of Government of India to exercise caution in 
this regard. In case where there are more than one Notifications under 
a single file, the Ministries/Departments should ensure that the file 
number on each notification should be suitably distinguished from one 
another.‖ 
(152nd Report) 
(Presented on 26th August, 2004)  
 
 
Time limit for rule making process 
  
 ―The Committee feels that the rule -making process could well 
have been completed within the time limit of six months which the 
Committee has laid down for making of rules under an Act after 
coming into force of that  Act. ................. Where however, a Ministry 
does not find it possible to adhere to the time limit, they should inform 
the Committee of the reasons why they cannot make rules within the 
time limit and seek extension of time for completing the rule -making 
process.‖ 
(47th  Report) 
(Presented on 14th September, 1981) 
             ―In addition to the concern expressed by the Committee over 
delay in laying of Notifications on the Table of the House, the 
Committee also express their deep concern over delay i n framing of 
rules/regulations under different Acts. The Committee in its 47 th report 
presented to the House on 14.9.1981 has recommended that the rule -
making process should be completed within a period of six months of 
the coming into force of an Act. The  Committee has further 
recommended that in case a Ministry is not able to adhere to this time 

49 
 
limit, it should seek extension of time for completing the rule -making 
process. The recommendation of the committee reads as follows:- 
               ―The Committee feels that the rule -making process could well 
have been completed within the time limit of six months which the 
Committee has laid down for making of rules under an Act after 
coming into force of that Act. Where however, a Ministry does not find 
it poss ible to adhere to the time limit, they should inform the 
Committee of the reasons why they cannot make rules within the time 
limit and seek extension of time for completing the rule -making 
process.‖ 
            The Committee is constrained to take note of the fact that the 
Ministries/Departments of Government of India are not complying 
with the Committee‘s recommendation to complete the process of 
framing of subordinate legislation contemplated under various Acts of 
Parliament within a period of six months.  At the same time, the 
Committee also does not receive requests for extension of time for 
completing the rule-making process in terms of its recommendations as 
above from most of the Ministries/Departments. The Committee takes 
a serious note of the prevail ing situation in which the 
Ministries/Departments are neither able to complete the task of 
framing rules/regulations under different Acts within the given six 
months‘ period nor do they suo-motu approach the Committee for 
seeking extension of time. The Committee further notes that in order to 
ensure timely framing of rules/regulations it has taken an initiative to 
monitor the progress of framing of subordinate legislation in respect of 
all legislations brought about by different Ministries/Departments of 
Government of India from the year 2003 onwards with a view to ensure 
that the framing of subordinate legislation is not delayed. The 
Committee, however, finds that the responses from the 
Ministries/Departments have not been prompt in this monitoring 
exercise undertaken on behalf of the Committee. 
 
            In view of the above position, the Committee once again 
reiterates its recommendation that the Ministries/Departments should 
ensure putting in place subordinate legislation positively within a 
period of six months of coming into force of the Ac t. The Committee 
further recommends that in case the Ministry fails to meet with this 
deadline, they should invariably approach the Committee for seeking 
extension of time. The Committee further recommends that in cases 
where the time limit of six months i s not adhered to, they should, while 
laying rules/regulations before the Parliament invariably lay a 
statement explaining the reasons why the framing of rules/regulations 
was delayed beyond six months so as to keep the Parliament apprised 

50 
 
of the compliance  of the Parliament‘s mandate   conferred upon the 
Executive.‖ 
(192nd Report) 
(Presented on 26th August, 2011)  
 
 The Committee observes that the whole process of enacting laws 
is defeated if rules are not framed and laid on the Table of the House in 
time.  A reasonable delay could be understood but unjustifiably long 
delays should be avoided and the Ministries should work towards 
improving this position.   The Committee hopes that Ministry would 
strive to keep the delay to the minimum and take care that 
Notifications are laid on the Table of the House at the first available 
opportunity after publication of Notifications. 
  The Committee would like to impress upon the Ministry that it 
is their responsibility to ensure that copies of the Notifications are 
printed timely and for this purpose, they need to evolve a suitable 
mechanism to ensure that notifications are laid in time.    The 
Committee is happy that the Ministry has evolved an internal 
mechanism/ procedure for this purpose.   The Committee hopes that it 
would take action accordingly in order to comply with the prescribed 
schedule in the matter of laying of statutory Notifications. 
 
The Ministry must take appropriate step to remove lacuna, if 
any, administrative or otherwise, causing such long delay by being  little 
proactive and prompt.   Further, the Ministries should take it up with 
the Ministry of Urban Development for late receiving of printed copies 
of the Notification. 
212th Report 
(Presented on 3rd September, 2013) 
  
 
Planning to lay papers during the initial stages of the Session 
 
         "The Committee recommends that Ministries should plan to lay 
their papers during the initial/middle stages of the session rather than 
scheduling this work towards the close of the session in order to avoid 
such happenings." 
173rd  Report 
(Presented on 22nd April, 2008) 
 
 

51 
 
Uploading Notifications on websites 
             Rules/Regulations/Orders/bye-laws are laid by different 
Ministries on the Table of the House under various provisions of parent 
Acts passed by Parliament. These Rules/ Regulations/Orders/bye -laws 
are published in the official Gazette before being laid on the Table of 
the House. Even after they are laid, it is very difficult to get access to 
these Notifications. The Committee recommends that such 
Rules/Regulations/Orders, etc. laid on the Table of the House should be 
invariably uploaded on the websites of respective Ministries 
immediately on the same day when they have been laid on the Table of 
the House, for information of Members of Parliament an d people at 
large for their easy access. 
 201st Report 
(Presented on 14th December, 2012)  
 
  
Late availability of printed copies 
             ―The Committee is not inclined to accept the late availability of 
printed copies of Notifications from the Government of India Press as 
the reason for delay in laying of the Notifications. The Committee 
directs all the Ministries that in case t here is a delay in procuring 
printed copies in time, they may obtain G.S.R. and S.O. numbers from 
the Government of India Press and prepare requisite number of copies 
in cyclostyled form for laying in the Parliament. The Committee also 
recommends that this  process may be adopted by Ministries suo-moto 
on immediate basis so as to avoid such delays henceforth‖. 
213th Report 
(Presented on 19th February, 2014) 
 
 
           The Committee recommends that all the Ministries/Departments 
should follow the above recommendations meticulously.
